### PlaceholderAPI Expansion Management Commands

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Commands

Commands for managing currently installed PlaceholderAPI expansions, including retrieving information, listing active expansions, and manual registration/unregistration.

```APIDOC
/papi info <expansion>
  Description: Gives you information about the specified Expansion.
  Arguments:
    <expansion>: The Expansion to get info from (Needs to be registered and active).
  Example:
    ```
    /papi info Vault
    ```

/papi list
  Description: Lists all active/registered expansions. This is different to /papi ecloud list installed in the fact, that it also includes expansions that were installed through a plugin (That aren't a separate jar-file) and it also doesn't show which one have updates available.

/papi register <filename>
  Description: Registers an expansion from a specified filename. This is useful in cases, where you downloaded the expansion manually and don't want to restart the server. The file needs to be inside /plugins/PlaceholderAPI/expansions.
  Arguments:
    <filename>: The file to register (including the file-extension).
  Example:
    ```
    /papi register MyExpansion.jar
    ```

/papi unregister <filename>
  Description: Unregisters the specified expansion.
  Arguments:
    <filename>: The expansion to unregister.
  Example:
    ```
    /papi unregister MyExpansion.jar
    ```
```

--------------------------------

### PlaceholderAPI E-Cloud Command Line Interface

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Expansion-cloud

Documentation for various PlaceholderAPI commands used to interact with the expansion cloud, including downloading, getting information about, and reloading expansions. These commands are executed in-game or via the server console.

```APIDOC
Command: /papi ecloud download <expansion> [version]
  Description: Downloads a specific expansion or a specific version of an expansion from the ecloud.
  Parameters:
    <expansion>: The name of the expansion to download (e.g., 'LuckPerms').
    [version]: (Optional) The specific version of the expansion to download (e.g., '1.2.3'). If omitted, the latest verified version is downloaded.
  Returns: Confirmation of download or error message.
  Usage:
    /papi ecloud download LuckPerms
    /papi ecloud download PlayerPoints 1.2.3
```

```APIDOC
Command: /papi ecloud info <expansion>
  Description: Retrieves information about an expansion, including available versions.
  Parameters:
    <expansion>: The name of the expansion to query (e.g., 'Vault').
  Returns: A list of available versions and other expansion details.
  Usage:
    /papi ecloud info Vault
```

```APIDOC
Command: /papi ecloud versioninfo <expansion> <version>
  Description: Provides detailed information about a specific version of an expansion.
  Parameters:
    <expansion>: The name of the expansion (e.g., 'Essentials').
    <version>: The specific version number to get details for (e.g., '2.18.2').
  Returns: Detailed information for the specified expansion version.
  Usage:
    /papi ecloud versioninfo Essentials 2.18.2
```

```APIDOC
Command: /papi reload
  Description: Reloads all installed PlaceholderAPI expansions, applying any changes or newly downloaded expansions. This command is crucial after downloading new expansions or updating existing ones.
  Parameters: None
  Returns: Confirmation of reload completion.
  Usage:
    /papi reload
```

### PlaceholderAPI Console Commands

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Using-Placeholders

These commands are used to manage PlaceholderAPI and its expansions directly from the server console or in-game. They allow for downloading new expansions, reloading the plugin to apply changes, and listing currently loaded expansions.

```APIDOC
/papi ecloud download <expansion_name>
  - Downloads a placeholder expansion from the PlaceholderAPI eCloud.
  - Parameters:
    - <expansion_name>: The name of the expansion to download (e.g., 'Player').
  - Usage: Used to install expansions not bundled with a plugin. Often followed by '/papi reload'.

/papi reload
  - Reloads PlaceholderAPI, applying any changes such as newly downloaded expansions or configuration updates.
  - No parameters.

/papi list
  - Lists all currently loaded placeholder expansions.
  - No parameters.
```

### Import PlaceholderAPI with Gradle

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Hook-into-PlaceholderAPI

For Gradle projects, configure your `build.gradle` file to include the PlaceholderAPI Maven repository and add the `compileOnly` dependency. Remember to substitute `{VERSION}` with the appropriate PlaceholderAPI version.

```Gradle
repositories {
    maven {
        url = 'https://repo.extendedclip.com/content/repositories/placeholderapi/'
    }
}

dependencies {
    compileOnly 'me.clip:placeholderapi:{VERSION}'
}
```

### Configure PlaceholderAPI as Soft Dependency in paper-plugin.yml

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Hook-into-PlaceholderAPI

To make PlaceholderAPI an optional server dependency in `paper-plugin.yml`, set `required: false` under its entry within the `dependencies.server` section. This ensures PlaceholderAPI loads before your plugin, but its absence won't prevent your plugin from starting.

```YAML
name: ExamplePlugin
version: 1.0
author: author
main: your.main.path.Here

dependencies:
    server:
      PlaceholderAPI:
        # Load order is relative to the dependency. So here PlaceholderAPI loads before our plugin.
        load: BEFORE
        required: false
```

### Configure PlaceholderAPI as Hard Dependency in plugin.yml

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Hook-into-PlaceholderAPI

To make PlaceholderAPI a mandatory dependency, list it under `depend` in your `plugin.yml`. Your plugin will only load if PlaceholderAPI is present and loaded on the server.

```YAML
name: ExamplePlugin
version: 1.0
author: author
main: your.main.path.Here

# This sets PlaceholderAPI as a dependency for your plugin.
depend: [PlaceholderAPI]
```

### Import PlaceholderAPI with Maven

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Hook-into-PlaceholderAPI

To include PlaceholderAPI in your Maven project, add its repository and dependency to your `pom.xml` file. Ensure you replace `{VERSION}` with the specific PlaceholderAPI version you intend to use.

```XML
    <repositories>
        <repository>
            <id>placeholderapi</id>
            <url>https://repo.extendedclip.com/content/repositories/placeholderapi/</url>
        </repository>
    </repositories>
    <dependencies>
        <dependency>
         <groupId>me.clip</groupId>
          <artifactId>placeholderapi</artifactId>
          <version>{VERSION}</version>
         <scope>provided</scope>
        </dependency>
    </dependencies>
```

### Integrate PlaceholderAPI for Dynamic Join Messages in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Hook-into-PlaceholderAPI

This Java code demonstrates how to integrate PlaceholderAPI into a Bukkit plugin. It checks for PlaceholderAPI's presence upon plugin enablement, registers an event listener for player join events, and uses `PlaceholderAPI.setPlaceholders()` to parse dynamic placeholders like player name and Vault rank into a custom join message. The example also includes robust error handling for scenarios where PlaceholderAPI is not installed, preventing plugin issues.

```java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi;

import me.clip.placeholderapi.PlaceholderAPI;

import org.bukkit.Bukkit;
import org.bukkit.event.EventHandler;
import org.bukkit.event.EventPriority;
import org.bukkit.event.Listener;
import org.bukkit.event.player.PlayerJoinEvent;
import org.bukkit.plugin.java.JavaPlugin;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.PlaceholderAPI;

public class JoinExample extends JavaPlugin implements Listener {

    @Override
    public void onEnable() {
 
        if (Bukkit.getPluginManager().getPlugin("PlaceholderAPI") != null) {
            /*
             * We register the EventListener here, when PlaceholderAPI is installed.
             * Since all events are in the main class (this class), we simply use "this"
             */
            Bukkit.getPluginManager().registerEvents(this, this);
        } else {
            /*
             * We inform about the fact that PlaceholderAPI isn't installed and then
             * disable this plugin to prevent issues.
             */
            getLogger().warn("Could not find PlaceholderAPI! This plugin is required.");
            Bukkit.getPluginManager().disablePlugin(this);
        }
    }

    @EventHandler(priority = EventPriority.HIGHEST)
    public void onJoin(PlayerJoinEvent event) {
        String joinText = "%player_name% &ajoined the server! They are rank &f%vault_rank%";

        /*
         * We parse the placeholders using "setPlaceholders"
         * This would turn %vault_rank% into the name of the Group, that the
         * joining player has.
         */
        joinText = PlaceholderAPI.setPlaceholders(event.getPlayer(), joinText);

        event.setJoinMessage(joinText);
    }
}
```

### Basic PlaceholderExpansion Structure in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/PlaceholderExpansion

This snippet provides the fundamental structure for a PlaceholderAPI expansion in Java, demonstrating the required methods for author, identifier, and version information. It serves as a starting point for custom placeholder implementations.

```Java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.expansions;

import org.bukkit.OfflinePlayer;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.PlaceholderExpansion;

public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {

    @Override
    public String getAuthor() {
        return "someauthor";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getIdentifier() {
        return "example";
    }

    @Override
    public String getVersion() {
        return "1.0.0";
    }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Complete PlaceholderAPI Expansion Implementation (Java)

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/PlaceholderExpansion

This comprehensive example illustrates a full PlaceholderExpansion class. It includes overriding essential methods like getAuthor, getIdentifier, getVersion, and persist. The onRequest method demonstrates how to process placeholder requests, access plugin configuration, and return dynamic values based on player input.

```java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.expansions;

import at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.SomePlugin;
import org.bukkit.OfflinePlayer;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.PlaceholderExpansion;

public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {

    private final SomePlugin plugin;
    
    public SomeExpansion(SomePlugin plugin) {
        this.plugin = plugin;
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getAuthor() {
        return "someauthor";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getIdentifier() {
        return "example";
    }

    @Override
    public String getVersion() {
        return "1.0.0";
    }
    
    @Override
    public boolean persist() {
        return true; // This is required or else PlaceholderAPI will unregister the Expansion on reload
    }
    
    @Override
    public String onRequest(OfflinePlayer player, String params) {
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder1")){
            return plugin.getConfig().getString("placeholders.placeholder1", "default1");
        }
        
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder2")) {
            return plugin.getConfig().getString("placeholders.placeholder2", "default2");
        }
        
        return null; // Placeholder is unknown by the Expansion
    }
}
```

### Configure PlaceholderAPI as Soft Dependency in plugin.yml

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Hook-into-PlaceholderAPI

To establish PlaceholderAPI as a required server dependency in `paper-plugin.yml`, set `required: true` under its entry within the `dependencies.server` section. This ensures PlaceholderAPI loads before your plugin and is essential for your plugin's operation.

### PlaceholderAPI Command-Line Interface (CLI) Reference

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/FAQ

This section details the command-line interface (CLI) commands available for managing PlaceholderAPI, including expansion management, parsing, and troubleshooting.

```APIDOC
/papi ecloud download <expansion>
  - Downloads a PlaceholderAPI expansion from the eCloud.
  - Parameters:
    - <expansion>: The name of the expansion to download.
  - Usage: Used to install new placeholder sets.

/papi reload
  - Reloads PlaceholderAPI, applying changes like newly downloaded expansions.
  - Usage: Essential after installing or removing expansions.

/papi parse me %placeholder%
  - Parses a specific placeholder for the current player.
  - Parameters:
    - %placeholder%: The placeholder string to parse (e.g., %player_name%).
  - Usage: Useful for debugging and verifying placeholder functionality.

/papi ecloud placeholders <expansion>
  - Lists all available placeholders for a specific expansion from the eCloud.
  - Parameters:
    - <expansion>: The name of the expansion to query.
  - Usage: Helps in discovering available placeholders and checking for typos.
```

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Using-Placeholders

This snippet demonstrates the standard format for embedding placeholders into plugin configurations or chat messages. Placeholders are typically enclosed within percent signs, allowing plugins to parse and replace them with dynamic values.

```Text
%placeholder_name%

Example: %player_name%
```

### Pinger Expansion

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

The Pinger expansion allows checking the online status and retrieving information from a server via its IP address or domain and port. Note that placeholders have a warmup time after installation and a separate update delay configurable in PlaceholderAPI's config.yml.

```APIDOC
%pinger_gameversion_<address>% - Retrieves the game version of the server.
%pinger_version_<address>% - Retrieves the full version string of the server.
%pinger_online_<address>% - Checks if the server is online (returns true/false).
%pinger_isonline_<address>% - Checks if the server is online (identical to %pinger_online_<address>%).
%pinger_max_<address>% - Retrieves the maximum number of players the server can hold.
%pinger_players_<address>% - Retrieves the current number of players online on the server.
%pinger_motd_<address>% - Retrieves the Message of the Day (MOTD) of the server.
%pinger_pingversion_<address>% - Retrieves the ping protocol version of the server.
  - Parameters:
    - <address>: Required. The IP address or domain and port of the server (e.g., 'testplugins.com:25565').
  - Note: Replace 'testplugins.com:25565' with your target server address.
```

--------------------------------

### Create a Relational PlaceholderAPI Expansion in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/PlaceholderExpansion

This Java example demonstrates how to implement a relational PlaceholderAPI expansion. By extending `PlaceholderExpansion` and implementing the `Relational` interface, it enables handling placeholders that involve two players, using the `onPlaceholderRequest(Player, Player, String)` method to define custom logic based on the relationship between them.

```Java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.expansions;

import at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.SomePlugin;
import org.bukkit.ChatColor;
import org.bukkit.Player;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.PlaceholderExpansion;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.Relational;

public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion implements Relational {

    private final SomePlugin plugin;
    
    public SomeExpansion(SomePlugin plugin) {
        this.plugin = plugin;
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getAuthor() {
        return "someauthor";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getIdentifier() {
        return "example";
    }

    @Override
    public String getVersion() {
        return "1.0.0";
    }
    
    @Override
    public boolean persist() {
        return true; // This is required or else PlaceholderAPI will unregister the Expansion on reload
    }
    
    @Override
    public String onPlaceholderRequest(Player one, Player two, String identifier) {
        if(one == null || two == null)
            return null; // We require both Players to be online
            
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("friend")) {
            if(plugin.areFriends(one, two)) {
                return ChatColor.GREEN + one.getName() + " and " + two.getName() + " are friends!";
            } else {
                return ChatColor.GREEN + one.getName() + " and " + two.getName() + " are not friends!";
            }
        }
        
        return null; // Placeholder is unknown by the Expansion
    }
}
```

### Declare PlaceholderAPI as Hard Dependency in Paper paper-plugin.yml

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Hook-into-PlaceholderAPI

Set PlaceholderAPI as a required dependency in your "paper-plugin.yml" for PaperMC projects. This ensures your plugin will only load if PlaceholderAPI is installed, specifying load order and mandatory requirement.

```YAML
name: ExamplePlugin
version: 1.0
author: author
main: your.main.path.Here

dependencies:
  server:
    PlaceholderAPI:
      # Load order is relative to the dependency. So here PlaceholderAPI loads before our plugin.
      load: BEFORE
      required: true
```

### Create Standalone PlaceholderAPI Expansion in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/PlaceholderExpansion

This Java example demonstrates how to implement a basic PlaceholderAPI expansion that operates independently without requiring any external plugins. It overrides core methods like `getAuthor`, `getIdentifier`, `getVersion`, and `onRequest` to provide static or player-specific placeholder values based on the requested parameter. This type of expansion is suitable for values derivable directly from the server or Java itself.

```Java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.expansions;

import org.bukkit.OfflinePlayer;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.PlaceholderExpansion;

public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {

    @Override
    public String getAuthor() {
        return "someauthor";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getIdentifier() {
        return "example";
    }

    @Override
    public String getVersion() {
        return "1.0.0";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String onRequest(OfflinePlayer player, String params) {
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("name")) {
            return player == null ? null : player.getName(); // "name" requires the player to be valid
        }
        
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder1")) {
            return "Placeholder Text 1";
        }
        
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder2")) {
            return "Placeholder Text 2";
        }
        
        return null; // Placeholder is unknown by the Expansion
    }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Full PlaceholderAPI Expansion with External Plugin Dependency in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/PlaceholderExpansion

This comprehensive Java example showcases a PlaceholderAPI expansion designed to integrate with an external plugin. It demonstrates overriding `getRequiredPlugin()` to declare the dependency and `canRegister()` to acquire a reference to the dependent plugin. The `onRequest` method then leverages this plugin instance to dynamically retrieve placeholder values, such as configuration strings, from the external source.

```java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.expansions;

import at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.SomePlugin;
import org.bukkit.OfflinePlayer;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.PlaceholderExpansion;

public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {

    private SomePlugin plugin; // This instance is assigned in canRegister()
    
    @Override
    public String getAuthor() {
        return "someauthor";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getIdentifier() {
        return "example";
    }

    @Override
    public String getVersion() {
        return "1.0.0";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getRequiredPlugin() {
        return "SomePlugin";
    }
    
    @Override
    public boolean canRegister() {
        return (plugin = (SomePlugin) Bukkit.getPluginManager().getPlugin(getRequiredPlugin())) != null;
    }
    
    @Override
    public String onRequest(OfflinePlayer player, String params) {
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder1")){
            return plugin.getConfig().getString("placeholders.placeholder1", "default1");
        }
        
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder2")){
            return plugin.getConfig().getString("placeholders.placeholder2", "default2");
        }
        
        return null; // Placeholder is unknown by the expansion
    }
}
```

--------------------------------

### Implement Plugin-Dependent PlaceholderAPI Expansion in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/PlaceholderExpansion

This comprehensive Java example showcases the development of a PlaceholderAPI expansion that relies on an external Bukkit plugin for its data. It demonstrates the use of `getRequiredPlugin()` to declare the dependency and `canRegister()` to acquire an instance of the dependent plugin. The `onRequest()` method then utilizes this plugin instance to fetch dynamic placeholder values, such as configuration strings, from the external plugin.

```Java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.expansions;

import at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.SomePlugin;
import org.bukkit.OfflinePlayer;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.PlaceholderExpansion;

public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {

    private SomePlugin plugin; // This instance is assigned in canRegister()
    
    @Override
    public String getAuthor() {
        return "someauthor";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getIdentifier() {
        return "example";
    }

    @Override
    public String getVersion() {
        return "1.0.0";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getRequiredPlugin() {
        return "SomePlugin";
    }
    
    @Override
    public boolean canRegister() {
        return (plugin = (SomePlugin) Bukkit.getPluginManager().getPlugin(getRequiredPlugin())) != null;
    }
    
    @Override
    public String onRequest(OfflinePlayer player, String params) {
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder1")){
            return plugin.getConfig().getString("placeholders.placeholder1", "default1");
        }
        
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder2")){
            return plugin.getConfig().getString("placeholders.placeholder2", "default2");
        }
        
        return null; // Placeholder is unknown by the expansion
    }
}
```

### ThemePark Plugin Placeholders and Installation

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Integrates with the ThemePark plugin to display attraction-specific information such as name, status, region, and ride counts. Supports filtering ride counts by position and type (daily, weekly, monthly, yearly, total). Includes the command to download the plugin.

```Bash
/papi ecloud download ThemePark
```

```APIDOC
%tp_name:<AttractionID>% - Name of the attraction. Replace <AttractionID> with the ID of your attraction.
%tp_status:<AttractionID>% - Status of the attraction. Replace <AttractionID> with the ID of your attraction.
%tp_region:<AttractionID>% - Region of the attraction. Replace <AttractionID> with the ID of your attraction.
%tp_ridecount% - Total ride count.
%tp_ridecount:<AttractionID>% - Ride count for a specific attraction. Replace <AttractionID> with the ID of your attraction.
%tp_ridecounttop_name:<AttractionID>:<Position>[:Type]% - Name of the top rider for an attraction. Replace <AttractionID> with the ID of your attraction. Replace <Position> with the ridecount position. Replace [Type] with the top type. Supported values: DAILY, WEEKLY, MONTHLY, YEARLY, TOTAL.
%tp_ridecounttop_value:<AttractionID>:<Position>[:Type]% - Value of the top rider for an attraction. Replace <AttractionID> with the ID of your attraction. Replace <Position> with the ridecount position. Replace [Type] with the top type. Supported values: DAILY, WEEKLY, MONTHLY, YEARLY, TOTAL.
```

### Example: Setting Player Join Message with PlaceholderAPI in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Hook-into-PlaceholderAPI

This Java code demonstrates how to integrate PlaceholderAPI into a Bukkit plugin to parse placeholders in a player's join message. It includes logic to check for PlaceholderAPI's presence, register an event listener, and use `PlaceholderAPI.setPlaceholders()` to replace dynamic placeholders like `%player_name%` and `%vault_rank%` with actual player-specific data.

```Java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi;

import me.clip.placeholderapi.PlaceholderAPI;

import org.bukkit.Bukkit;
import org.bukkit.event.EventHandler;
import org.bukkit.event.EventPriority;
import org.bukkit.event.Listener;
import org.bukkit.event.player.PlayerJoinEvent;
import org.bukkit.plugin.java.JavaPlugin;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.PlaceholderAPI;

public class JoinExample extends JavaPlugin implements Listener {

    @Override
    public void onEnable() {
 
        if (Bukkit.getPluginManager().getPlugin("PlaceholderAPI") != null) {
            /*
             * We register the EventListener here, when PlaceholderAPI is installed.
             * Since all events are in the main class (this class), we simply use "this"
             */
            Bukkit.getPluginManager().registerEvents(this, this);
        } else {
            /*
             * We inform about the fact that PlaceholderAPI isn't installed and then
             * disable this plugin to prevent issues.
             */
            getLogger().warn("Could not find PlaceholderAPI! This plugin is required.");
            Bukkit.getPluginManager().disablePlugin(this);
        }
    }

    @EventHandler(priority = EventPriority.HIGHEST)
    public void onJoin(PlayerJoinEvent event) {
        String joinText = "%player_name% &ajoined the server! They are rank &f%vault_rank%";

        /*
         * We parse the placeholders using "setPlaceholders"
         * This would turn %vault_rank% into the name of the Group, that the
         * joining player has.
         */
        joinText = PlaceholderAPI.setPlaceholders(event.getPlayer(), joinText);

        event.setJoinMessage(joinText);
    }
}
```

### PlaceholderAPI Expansion Cloud Management Commands

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Expansion-cloud

Commands for interacting with the PlaceholderAPI expansion cloud directly from the server console or in-game. These commands allow downloading, querying information about, and reloading expansions.

```APIDOC
/papi ecloud download <expansion> [version]
  - Downloads a specified expansion from the PlaceholderAPI expansion cloud.
  - Parameters:
    - <expansion>: The name of the expansion to download.
    - [version]: (Optional) The specific version of the expansion to download. If omitted, the latest verified version is downloaded.
  - Usage:
    - /papi ecloud download Player
    - /papi ecloud download Player 1.0.0

/papi ecloud info <expansion>
  - Displays information about an expansion, including available versions.
  - Parameters:
    - <expansion>: The name of the expansion to query.
  - Usage:
    - /papi ecloud info Player

/papi ecloud versioninfo <expansion> <version>
  - Provides detailed information about a specific version of an expansion.
  - Parameters:
    - <expansion>: The name of the expansion.
    - <version>: The specific version to get info about.
  - Usage:
    - /papi ecloud versioninfo Player 1.0.0

/papi reload
  - Reloads PlaceholderAPI and refreshes installed expansions.
  - Usage:
    - /papi reload
```

--------------------------------

```APIDOC
Parameters:
  StatsType: 'kills', 'deaths', 'combatlogs', 'points'.
  Rank: A number between 1 to the pull limit.
  Group: The _servergroup_.
```

```APIDOC
%skillapi_attribute_points%
%skillapi_mana%
%skillapi_max_mana%
%skillapi_skill_level_<skillName>%
%skillapi_skill_points_<skillName>%
%skillapi_skill_cost_<skillName>%
%skillapi_skill_levelreq_<skillName>%
%skillapi_skill_is_maxed_<skillName>%
%skillapi_skill_message_<skillName>%
%skillapi_skill_type_<skillName>%
%skillapi_skill_req_<skillName>%

The following placeholders are the same as above but instead of specifying the skillName, you can specify a number from 1 to pretty much infinity (amount of skills a player has) which will show the information related to the players 1st, 2nd, 3rd skill and so on...
%skillapi_player_skill_points_<#>%
%skillapi_player_skill_level_<#>%
%skillapi_player_skill_levelreq_<#>%
%skillapi_player_skill_name_<#>%
%skillapi_player_skill_message_<#>%
%skillapi_player_skill_req_<#>%
%skillapi_player_skill_type_<#>%
%skillapi_player_skill_can_autolevel_<#>%
%skillapi_player_skill_can_cast_<#>%
```

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

Placeholders for the OpEconomy plugin, allowing retrieval of player money, baltop information, currency, and details about active codes.

```PlaceholderAPI

%opeconomy_money% - Gets money

%opeconomy_baltop_player_position% - Getting position of player
%opeconomy_currency% - Shows active currency
%opeconomy_is_active_code_<code>% - Boolean, if code is active
%opeconomy_max_uses_code_<code>% - Shows max uses for code
%opeconomy_uses_code_<code>% - Shows uses for code
%opeconomy_reward_code_<code>% - Shows reward for entering code
```

### PlaceholderAPI Server Information Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

```APIDOC

```

### PlaceholderAPI Command-Line Interface (CLI) Usage

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/FAQ

Common server commands used to manage PlaceholderAPI expansions, parse placeholders, and check plugin statuses. These commands are executed in the server console or in-game.

```Minecraft Command
/papi ecloud download <expansion>
/papi reload
/papi parse me %placeholder%
/papi ecloud placeholders <expansion>
/pl
```

### PlaceholderAPI Expansion Cloud Configuration

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Expansion-cloud

Configuration options found in config.yml and environment variables that control PlaceholderAPI's interaction with the expansion cloud.

```APIDOC
cloud_enabled (config.yml)
  - Type: boolean
  - Default: true
  - Description: Controls whether PlaceholderAPI connects to the expansion cloud on server startup. Set to 'false' to disable cloud connection.

cloud_allow_unverified_expansions (config.yml)
  - Type: boolean
  - Default: false
  - Description: Allows downloading unverified expansions via PlaceholderAPI commands. If 'false', only verified expansions can be downloaded through commands. Unverified expansions can still be downloaded manually from the website.

PAPI_BLOCKED_EXPANSIONS (Environment Variable)
  - Type: string (comma-separated list)
  - Description: A comma-separated list of expansion names that should be blocked from being downloaded by PlaceholderAPI.
  - Example: PAPI_BLOCKED_EXPANSIONS=Player,Server
```

### Math PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Documentation for the Math expansion, which allows performing simple or advanced mathematical calculations using EvalEx syntax, supporting any placeholder that returns a number.

```APIDOC
%math_<math expression>%
  - Evaluates a mathematical expression.
    - <math expression>: Any valid mathematical expression supported by EvalEx (e.g., '2 + 2 * 3', 'sqrt(9)'). Use '[prc]' instead of '%' for modulo.
%math_[precision]:[rounding]_<expression>%
  - Evaluates a mathematical expression with specified precision and rounding mode.
    - [precision]: Optional number of decimal places for the result.
    - [rounding]: Optional rounding mode (e.g., 'HALF_UP', 'CEILING').
    - <expression>: The mathematical expression to evaluate.
```

### PlaceholderAPI Console and In-Game Commands

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Using-Placeholders

This section details the primary commands for managing PlaceholderAPI expansions and reloading the plugin, along with the general format for using placeholders. These commands are executable from the server console or in-game with appropriate permissions.

```APIDOC
PlaceholderAPI Command Line Interface (CLI) and Placeholder Format:

/papi ecloud download <expansion_name>
  - Description: Downloads a specified placeholder expansion from the PlaceholderAPI eCloud. This command is used when an expansion is not bundled with a plugin and needs to be fetched separately.
  - Parameters:
    - <expansion_name>: (string, required) The name of the expansion to download (e.g., 'Player', 'Server').
  - Usage Example: /papi ecloud download Player

/papi reload
  - Description: Reloads the PlaceholderAPI plugin. This command should be executed after downloading new expansions or making changes to PlaceholderAPI's configuration files to ensure they take effect.
  - Usage: Run after any expansion download or configuration modification.

/papi list
  - Description: Displays a list of all currently loaded and active PlaceholderAPI expansions on the server.
  - Returns: A textual list of loaded expansion names.
  - Usage: Use to verify which expansions are active and available for use.

Placeholder Format: %<identifier>_<placeholder_name>%
  - Description: The standard syntax for embedding placeholders within plugin configurations, chat messages, or other text fields that support PlaceholderAPI parsing. The system replaces these tags with dynamic data.
  - Components:
    - <identifier>: (string) A prefix that identifies the specific placeholder expansion (e.g., 'player' for player-related data, 'server' for server statistics).
    - <placeholder_name>: (string) The specific data point or function provided by the expansion (e.g., 'name' for player's name, 'online' for server's online player count).
  - Usage Example: %player_name% (will display the player's current name)
  - Note: Refer to individual plugin or expansion documentation for specific available placeholders and their identifiers.
```

### ChatReaction PlaceholderAPI Expansion

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

Documents the PlaceholderAPI expansion for ChatReaction, providing placeholders for tracking wins, player statistics, leaderboard data, reaction types, active rounds, and time-based information. Requires MySQL for some features.

```APIDOC
%chatreaction_wins% - Chat reaction wins (Requires MySQL)
%chatreaction_wins_<playername>% - Chat reactions wins for a certain player (Requires MySQL)
%chatreaction_top_player_<placement>% - Returns the player at the placement inputted from the leaderboard (Requires MySQL)
%chatreaction_top_wins_<placement>% - Returns the amount of wins a player has at that placement (Requires MySQL)
%chatreaction_type% - Type of reaction (Reaction or Scramble)
%chatreaction_active_round% - Is a round currently active
%chatreaction_display_word% - The scrambled word
%chatreaction_reaction_word% - The actual solution word
%chatreaction_start_time% - Time when the round started
%chatreaction_latest_winner% - Last recorded winner
%chatreaction_start_time_in_seconds% - Returns the time (in seconds) since the chat reaction started
%chatreaction_time_remaining% - returns the time (in seconds) when the chat reaction event will expire
```

### PlaceholderAPI eCloud Management Commands

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Commands

Commands for interacting with the PlaceholderAPI Expansion Cloud (eCloud), including cache management, connection control, expansion download, information retrieval, listing, and status checks.

```APIDOC
/papi ecloud clear
  Description: Clears the cache for the eCloud.

/papi ecloud disable
  Description: Disables the connection to the eCloud.

/papi ecloud download <expansion> [version]
  Description: Allows you to download an expansion from the eCloud.
  Arguments:
    <expansion>: The expansion to download.
    [version]: The specific version of the expansion to download (Optional).
  Example:
    ```
    /papi ecloud download Vault
    /papi ecloud download Vault 1.5.2
    ```

/papi ecloud enable
  Description: Enables the connection to the eCloud.

/papi ecloud info <expansion> [version]
  Description: Gives information about a specific Expansion.
  Arguments:
    <expansion>: The Expansion to retrieve information from.
    [version]: The Expansion's version to get information from.
  Example:
    ```
    /papi ecloud info Vault
    ```

/papi ecloud list <all|<author>|installed>
  Description: Lists either all Expansions on the eCloud, only those by a specific author or only those that you have installed. Installed Expansions show as green in the list and Expansions that are installed and have an update available show as gold.
  Arguments:
    <all|<author>|installed>: List all Expansions, Expansions of a specific author or all Expansions you have installed.
  Example:
    ```
    /papi ecloud list all
    /papi ecloud list clip
    /papi ecloud list installed
    ```

/papi ecloud placeholders <expansion>
  Description: List all placeholders of an Expansion.
  Arguments:
    <expansion>: The Expansion to list placeholders of.
  Example:
    ```
    /papi ecloud placeholders Vault
    ```

/papi ecloud refresh
  Description: Refresh the cached data from the eCloud.

/papi ecloud status
  Description: Displays the actual Status of the eCloud.
```

### GraalJS PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Documentation for the GraalJS expansion, allowing execution of GraalJS scripts via placeholders, with support for passing single or multiple arguments.

```APIDOC
%graaljs_<your_placeholder_identifier>%
  - Executes the GraalJS script associated with the identifier without arguments.
%graaljs_<your_placeholder_identifier>_arg1%
  - Executes the GraalJS script with a single argument.
    - arg1: The first argument to pass to the script.
%graaljs_<your_placeholder_identifier>_arg1,arg2,arg3%
  - Executes the GraalJS script with multiple comma-separated arguments.
    - arg1,arg2,arg3: Comma-separated arguments to pass to the script.
```

### SubServers PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

This section details the PlaceholderAPI placeholders available for the SubServers plugin, allowing retrieval of comprehensive information about proxies, hosts, servers, and subservers within a SubServers network. These placeholders are crucial for monitoring and displaying the status and details of your server infrastructure.

```APIDOC
%subservers_proxies()%
%subservers_proxy.displayname(<Proxy>)%
%subservers_proxy.type(<Proxy>)_{Master Proxy, Proxy}%
%subservers_proxy.players(<Proxy>)%
%subservers_proxy.subdata(<Proxy>)_{Connected, Disconnected}%
%subservers_proxy.subdata.channels(<Proxy>)%
%subservers_proxy.subdata.subchannels(<Proxy>)%
%subservers_proxy.signature(<Proxy>)%

%subservers_hosts()%
%subservers_host.displayname(<Host>)%
%subservers_host.available(<Host>)_{Available, Unavailable}%
%subservers_host.enabled(<Host>)_{Enabled, Disabled}%
%subservers_host.address(<Host>)%
%subservers_host.creator.templates(<Host>)%
%subservers_host.creator.template.displayname(<Host>, <Template>)%
%subservers_host.creator.template.enabled(<Host>, <Template>)_{Enabled, Disabled}%
%subservers_host.creator.template.type(<Host>, <Template>)%
%subservers_host.creator.template.requiresversion(<Host>, <Template>)_{Optional, Required}%
%subservers_host.creator.template.updatable(<Host>, <Template>)_{Updatable, Not Updatable}%
%subservers_host.subservers(<Host>)%
%subservers_host.players(<Host>)%
%subservers_host.subdata(<Host>)_{Connected, Unsupported, Disconnected}%
%subservers_host.subdata.channels(<Host>)%
%subservers_host.subdata.subchannels(<Host>)%
%subservers_host.signature(<Host>)%

%subservers_servers()%
%subservers_server.displayname(<Server>)%
%subservers_server.type(<Server>)_{Subserver, Server}%
%subservers_server.groups(<Server>)%
%subservers_server.address(<Server>)%
%subservers_server.motd(<Server>)%
%subservers_server.restricted(<Server>)_{Public, Private}%
%subservers_server.hidden(<Server>)_{Visible, Hidden}%
%subservers_server.players(<Server>)%
%subservers_server.subdata(<Server>)_{Connected, Disconnected}%
%subservers_server.subdata.channels(<Server>)%
%subservers_server.subdata.subchannels(<Server>)%
%subservers_server.signature(<Server>)%

%subservers_subservers()%
%subservers_subserver.displayname(<Subserver>)%
%subservers_subserver.available(<Subserver>)_{Available, Unavailable}%
```

### Reloading PlaceholderAPI Expansions via Command Line

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Common-Issues

After downloading or updating PlaceholderAPI expansions, it is crucial to execute the `/papi reload` command to ensure they are properly loaded and activated. This step is essential for new expansions to function correctly and for changes to take effect.

```CLI
/papi reload
```

### DeluxeMenus Plugin Placeholders (API)

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Comprehensive PlaceholderAPI documentation for DeluxeMenus plugin, detailing meta-data retrieval with type and default value parameters.

```APIDOC
%deluxemenus_meta_<key>_<dataType>_<default_value>%

Parameters:
  Key: The key of the meta you want to check
  Data Type: Can be 'STRING', 'BOOLEAN', 'DOUBLE', 'LONG', or 'INTEGER'.
    *If the given key has a different data type, an error will occur.*
  Default Value: The value returned if nothing is found.
```

### Create a Basic PlaceholderAPI Expansion in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/PlaceholderExpansion

This Java code demonstrates how to create a standard PlaceholderAPI expansion. It extends the `PlaceholderExpansion` class, implementing methods like `getAuthor`, `getIdentifier`, `getVersion`, `persist`, and `onRequest` to define the expansion's metadata and handle placeholder requests based on input parameters.

```Java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.expansions;

import at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.SomePlugin;
import org.bukkit.OfflinePlayer;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.PlaceholderExpansion;

public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {

    private final SomePlugin plugin;
    
    public SomeExpansion(SomePlugin plugin) {
        this.plugin = plugin;
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getAuthor() {
        return "someauthor";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getIdentifier() {
        return "example";
    }

    @Override
    public String getVersion() {
        return "1.0.0";
    }
    
    @Override
    public boolean persist() {
        return true; // This is required or else PlaceholderAPI will unregister the Expansion on reload
    }
    
    @Override
    public String onRequest(OfflinePlayer player, String params) {
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder1")){
            return plugin.getConfig().getString("placeholders.placeholder1", "default1");
        }
        
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder2")) {
            return plugin.getConfig().getString("placeholders.placeholder2", "default2");
        }
        
        return null; // Placeholder is unknown by the Expansion
    }
}
```

### DeluxeCombat PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

PlaceholderAPI placeholders for the DeluxeCombat plugin, providing detailed combat statistics and player combat status, including ranking metrics and current combat state.

```APIDOC

%deluxecombat_ranking_maxstreak%
%deluxecombat_in_combat%
```

### PlaceholderExpansion Core Methods API

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/PlaceholderExpansion

Documentation for the essential methods required when implementing a PlaceholderAPI `PlaceholderExpansion`, including author, identifier, and version information.

```APIDOC
getAuthor(): String
  - Purpose: Sets the name of the expansion's author.
  - Returns: The author's name as a String.

getIdentifier(): String
  - Purpose: Defines the unique identifier for the placeholder, used in the placeholder string (e.g., "%identifier_placeholder%").
  - Constraints: Cannot contain '%', '{', '}', or '_'.
  - Note: The `getName()` method can be overridden to display a different name if these symbols are needed.
  - Returns: The expansion's identifier as a String.

getVersion(): String
  - Purpose: Specifies the expansion's version string.
  - Usage: Used to determine if a new update is available when the expansion is shared on the eCloud.
  - Returns: The expansion's version as a String.
```

### PlaceholderAPI Advancements Expansion Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Provides placeholders related to player advancements, including completion status, lists, and amounts. This expansion can be downloaded using `/papi ecloud download Advancements`.


### Constructor-Based Dependency Injection for PlaceholderAPI Expansion in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/PlaceholderExpansion

This Java code snippet illustrates the use of constructor-based dependency injection to provide an instance of `SomePlugin` to a `PlaceholderExpansion`. This pattern promotes cleaner code and easier testing by allowing the plugin instance to be passed directly upon expansion creation, offering a more robust alternative to direct `PluginManager` lookups for internal plugin expansions.

```java
public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {
    private final SomePlugin plugin; // The instance is created in the constructor and won't be modified, so it can be final
    
    public SomeExpansion(SomePlugin plugin) {
        this.plugin = plugin;
    }
}
```

### PlaceholderExpansion Class Methods (Java API)

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/PlaceholderExpansion

Detailed API documentation for key methods within the PlaceholderExpansion class, essential for defining custom PlaceholderAPI expansions. It covers methods for setting expansion metadata (author, identifier, version) and handling placeholder requests for both online and offline players.

```APIDOC
PlaceholderExpansion Class Methods:

getAuthor(): String
  - Description: Sets the name of the expansion's author.
  - Returns: The author's name as a String.

getIdentifier(): String
  - Description: Defines the unique identifier for the placeholder, used between the first '%' and the first '_'. Cannot contain '%', '{', '}', or '_'.
  - Returns: The expansion's identifier as a String.
  - Notes: Can override getName() for different display name.

getVersion(): String
  - Description: Specifies the expansion's version. Used for update checks on the eCloud.
  - Returns: The expansion's version as a String.

onRequest(OfflinePlayer player, String identifier): String
  - Description: Recommended method for handling placeholder requests, allowing usage with offline players. Automatically calls onPlaceholderRequest(Player, String) if not explicitly set.
  - Parameters:
    - player: The OfflinePlayer associated with the request.
    - identifier: The specific placeholder identifier being requested.
  - Returns: The resolved placeholder value as a String, or null if invalid.

onPlaceholderRequest(Player player, String identifier): String
  - Description: Handles placeholder requests for online players. The Player object can be null.
  - Parameters:
    - player: The Player associated with the request (can be null).
    - identifier: The specific placeholder identifier being requested.
  - Returns: The resolved placeholder value as a String, or null if invalid.
```

--------------------------------

### PlaceholderAPI eCloud Commands

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Commands

Commands for interacting with the PlaceholderAPI Expansion Cloud, including managing connections, downloading expansions, and retrieving information about cloud-hosted expansions.

```APIDOC
/papi ecloud clear
  Description: Clears the cache for the eCloud.

/papi ecloud disable
  Description: Disables the connection to the eCloud.

/papi ecloud download <expansion> [version]
  Description: Allows you to download an expansion from the eCloud.
  Arguments:
    <expansion>: The expansion to download.
    [version]: The specific version of the expansion to download (Optional).
  Example:
    /papi ecloud download Vault
    /papi ecloud download Vault 1.5.2

/papi ecloud enable
  Description: Enables the connection to the eCloud.

/papi ecloud info <expansion> [version]
  Description: Gives information about a specific Expansion.
  Arguments:
    <expansion>: The Expansion to retrieve information from.
    [version]: The Expansion's version to get information from.
  Example:
    /papi ecloud info Vault

/papi ecloud list <all|<author>|installed>
  Description: Lists either all Expansions on the eCloud, only those by a specific author or only those that you have installed. Installed Expansions show as green in the list and Expansions that are installed and have an update available show as gold.
  Arguments:
    <all|<author>|installed>: List all Expansions, Expansions of a specific author or all Expansions you have installed.
  Example:
    /papi ecloud list all
    /papi ecloud list clip
    /papi ecloud list installed

/papi ecloud placeholders <expansion>
  Description: List all placeholders of an Expansion.
  Arguments:
    <expansion>: The Expansion to list placeholders of.
  Example:
    /papi ecloud placeholders Vault

/papi ecloud refresh
  Description: Refresh the cached data from the eCloud.

/papi ecloud status
  Description: Displays the actual Status of the eCloud.
```

### Basic PlaceholderAPI Expansion Implementation in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/PlaceholderExpansion

This Java code demonstrates a fundamental PlaceholderAPI expansion. It extends `PlaceholderExpansion` and overrides essential methods like `getAuthor`, `getIdentifier`, `getVersion`, and `onRequest` to define the expansion's metadata and implement logic for handling various placeholder requests, including player-specific data and static text.

```java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.expansions;

import org.bukkit.OfflinePlayer;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.PlaceholderExpansion;

public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {

    @Override
    public String getAuthor() {
        return "someauthor";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getIdentifier() {
        return "example";
    }

    @Override
    public String getVersion() {
        return "1.0.0";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String onRequest(OfflinePlayer player, String params) {
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("name")) {
            return player == null ? null : player.getName(); // "name" requires the player to be valid
        }
        
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder1")) {
            return "Placeholder Text 1";
        }
        
        if(params.equalsIgnoreCase("placeholder2")) {
            return "Placeholder Text 2";
        }
        
        return null; // Placeholder is unknown by the Expansion
    }
}
```

### PlaceholderAPI Parse Commands

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Commands

Comprehensive documentation for PlaceholderAPI commands used to parse and debug placeholders, including broadcasting, command execution, and relational parsing.

```APIDOC
/papi bcparse <player|me> <string>
  Description: Parses placeholders of a String and broadcasts the result to all players.
  Arguments:
    <player|me>: The Player to parse values of the placeholder (Use `me` for yourself).
    <Text with placeholders>: The text to parse.
  Example:
    /papi bcparse funnycube My name is %player_name%!

/papi cmdparse <player|me> <string>
  Description: Parses placeholders of a String and executes it as a command.
  Arguments:
    <player|me>: The player to parse placeholders against. Use `me` for yourself.
    <Command with placeholders>: The Text to parse and execute as command. Please leave away the `/` of the command.
  Example:
    /papi cmdparse funnycube say My name is %player_name%!

/papi parse <player|me> <string>
  Description: Parses the placeholders in a given text and shows the result.
  Arguments:
    <player|me>: The Player to parse values of the placeholder (Use `me` for yourself).
    <Text with placeholders>: The text to parse.
  Example:
    /papi parse funnycube My group is %vault_group%

/papi parserel <player1> <player2> <string>
  Description: Parses a relational placeholder.
  Arguments:
    <player1>: The first player.
    <player2>: The second player to compare with.
    <Text with placeholders>: The actual placeholder to parse.
  Example:
    /papi parserel funnycube extended_clip %placeholder%
```

### Formatter PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Documentation for the Formatter expansion, offering a variety of placeholders for number and text formatting, including number formatting, time unit conversions, rounding, shortening, and text manipulation.

```APIDOC
%formatter_number_format_<number>%
  - Formats a given number using default locale.
%formatter_number_format_[locale]:[pattern]_<number>%
  - Formats a given number with a specified locale and pattern.
    - [locale]: Optional locale (e.g., 'en_US').
    - [pattern]: Optional formatting pattern (e.g., '#,##0.00').
    - <number>: The number to format.
%formatter_number_from:<timeunit>_to:<timeunit>_<number>%
  - Converts a number from one time unit to another.
    - <timeunit>: Source and target time units (e.g., 'SECONDS', 'MINUTES', 'HOURS', 'DAYS').
    - <number>: The number to convert.
%formatter_number_round_<number>%
  - Rounds a given number to the nearest integer.
%formatter_number_[precision]:[roundingmode]_<number>%
  - Rounds a given number with specified precision and rounding mode.
    - [precision]: Optional number of decimal places.
    - [roundingmode]: Optional rounding mode (e.g., 'HALF_UP', 'CEILING').
    - <number>: The number to round.
%formatter_number_shorten_<number>%
  - Shortens a number (e.g., 1000000 becomes 1M).
%formatter_number_time_<number>%
  - Formats a number as a time duration (e.g., seconds into HH:MM:SS).
%formatter_number_time_<timeunit>_<number>%
  - Formats a number as a time duration, treating the number as the specified time unit.
    - <timeunit>: The unit of the input number (e.g., 'SECONDS', 'MILLISECONDS').
    - <number>: The number representing time.
%formatter_text_length_<text>%
  - Returns the length of the given text.
%formatter_text_lowercase_<text>%
  - Converts the given text to lowercase.
%formatter_text_replace_[target]_[replacement]_<text>%
  - Replaces all occurrences of a target string with a replacement string in the given text.
    - [target]: The substring to find.
    - [replacement]: The substring to replace with.
    - <text>: The input text.
%formatter_text_substring_[start]:[end]_<text>%
  - Extracts a substring from the given text based on start and end indices.
    - [start]: The starting index (inclusive).
    - [end]: The ending index (exclusive).
    - <text>: The input text.
%formatter_text_uppercase_<text>%
  - Converts the given text to uppercase.
```

--------------------------------

### Clans-API for Spigot PlaceholderAPI Expansion

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

Documents the PlaceholderAPI expansion for Clans-API, offering placeholders to display custom designed and standard clan tags in chat.


### Dependency Injection for PlaceholderAPI Expansion Constructor (Java)

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/PlaceholderExpansion

This snippet demonstrates how to implement dependency injection in a PlaceholderAPI expansion. It shows passing a SomePlugin instance to the SomeExpansion constructor, allowing the expansion to access plugin-specific methods and data. This approach ensures the plugin instance is available throughout the expansion's lifecycle.

```java
public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {
    private final SomePlugin plugin; // The instance is created in the constructor and won't be modified, so it can be final
    
    public SomeExpansion(SomePlugin plugin) {
        this.plugin = plugin;
    }
}
```

### CooldownBar PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Documentation for the CooldownBar expansion, providing various placeholders to display cooldown progress, symbols, length, and percentages. It allows for detailed customization of cooldown visualization.

```APIDOC
%cooldownbar_{placeholder}%
  - Displays a cooldown bar for the specified placeholder.
%cooldownbar_{placeholder}_p:<PassedTimeSymbol>%
  - Customizes the symbol for passed time.
%cooldownbar_{placeholder}_i:<InProgressSymbol>%
  - Customizes the symbol for in-progress time.
%cooldownbar_{placeholder}_r:<RemainingSymbol>%
  - Customizes the symbol for remaining time.
%cooldownbar_{placeholder}_l:<Maxlength>%
  - Sets the maximum length of the cooldown bar.
%cooldownbar_{placeholder}_c:<MainCooldown>%
  - Defines the main cooldown duration.
%cooldownbar_{placeholder}_rdy:<ReadyText>%
  - Sets the text to display when the cooldown is ready.
%cooldownbar_{essentials_kit_time_until_available_tools}_p:&a■_i:&e■_r:&7■_l:5_c:100_rdy:&aReadytoclaim!%
  - Example usage for an Essentials kit cooldown.
%cooldownbar_percentage_{placeholder}%
  - Displays the cooldown as a percentage.
%cooldownbar_percentage_{placeholder}_c:<MainCooldown>%
  - Defines the main cooldown duration for percentage calculation.
%cooldownbar_percentage_{placeholder}_d:<AmountOfDecimal>%
  - Sets the amount of decimal places for the percentage.
%cooldownbar_percentage_{essentials_kit_time_until_available_tools}_c:120_d:0%
  - Example usage for an Essentials kit cooldown percentage.
```

### PlaceholderAPI Parse Commands

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Commands

Commands for parsing placeholders in strings, useful for debugging and displaying dynamic content.

```APIDOC
/papi bcparse <player|me> <Text with placeholders>
  Description: Parses placeholders of a String and broadcasts the result to all players.
  Arguments:
    <player|me>: The Player to parse values of the placeholder (Use `me` for yourself).
    <Text with placeholders>: The text to parse.
  Example: /papi bcparse funnycube My name is %player_name%!

/papi cmdparse <player|me> <Command with placeholders>
  Description: Parses placeholders of a String and executes it as a command.
  Arguments:
    <player|me>: The player to parse placeholders against. Use `me` for yourself.
    <Command with placeholders>: The Text to parse and execute as command. Please leave away the `/` of the command.
  Example: /papi cmdparse funnycube say My name is %player_name%!

/papi parse <player|me> <Text with placeholders>
  Description: Parses the placeholders in a given text and shows the result.
  Arguments:
    <player|me>: The Player to parse values of the placeholder (Use `me` for yourself).
    <Text with placeholders>: The text to parse.
  Example: /papi parse funnycube My group is %vault_group%

/papi parserel <player1> <player2> <Text with placeholders>
  Description: Parses a relational placeholder.
  Arguments:
    <player1>: The first player.
    <player2>: The second player to compare with.
    <Text with placeholders>: The actual placeholder to parse.
  Example: /papi parserel funnycube extended_clip %placeholder%
```

### PlaceholderAPI PlayerList Expansion

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Allows creation and retrieval of custom player lists configured within PlaceholderAPI's configuration. Use this to display player lists or their counts dynamically.

```APIDOC
Download Command:
/papi ecloud download playerlist

Usage:
%playerlist_<listName>_<list|amount|#>%
  - <listName>: The name of the list defined in PAPI's config.
  - <list>: Displays the full list of players.
  - <amount>: Displays the total number of players in the list.
  - <#>: Displays a specific player from the list by their index (e.g., %playerlist_myList_1% for the first player).
```

### Basic PlaceholderExpansion Structure in Java

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/PlaceholderExpansion

This code snippet provides the fundamental structure for a custom PlaceholderAPI expansion, demonstrating the implementation of required methods like `getAuthor`, `getIdentifier`, and `getVersion`.

```Java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example.expansions;

import org.bukkit.OfflinePlayer;
import me.clip.placeholderapi.expansion.PlaceholderExpansion;

public class SomeExpansion extends PlaceholderExpansion {

    @Override
    public String getAuthor() {
        return "someauthor";
    }
    
    @Override
    public String getIdentifier() {
        return "example";
    }

    @Override
    public String getVersion() {
        return "1.0.0";
    }
}
```

### PlaceholderExpansion Request Handling Methods API

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/PlaceholderExpansion

Documentation for the primary methods used to handle placeholder requests within a PlaceholderAPI expansion, distinguishing between online and offline player contexts.

```APIDOC
onRequest(OfflinePlayer player, String identifier): String
  - Purpose: Recommended method for handling placeholder requests, allowing usage with offline players.
  - Parameters:
    - player: An `OfflinePlayer` instance, representing the player for whom the placeholder is being requested.
    - identifier: The specific part of the placeholder string after the expansion's main identifier (e.g., "name" in "%player_name%").
  - Behavior: If not explicitly set, this method automatically calls `onPlaceholderRequest(Player, String)`.
  - Returns: The processed placeholder value as a String, or `null` if the placeholder is invalid.

onPlaceholderRequest(Player player, String identifier): String
  - Purpose: Handles placeholder requests specifically for online players.
  - Parameters:
    - player: A `Player` instance, representing the online player. This can be `null`.
    - identifier: The specific part of the placeholder string after the expansion's main identifier.
  - Behavior: If not set, this method will return `null`, which PlaceholderAPI interprets as an invalid placeholder. Developers must account for `null` `Player` instances.
  - Returns: The processed placeholder value as a String, or `null` if the placeholder is invalid.
```

### PlaceholderAPI CheckItem Extension Commands and Modifiers

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

This section details the PlaceholderAPI CheckItem extension, which allows for advanced inventory checks, item manipulation (give/remove), and information retrieval. It outlines the primary placeholders and a comprehensive list of modifiers that can be combined to create specific conditions for item matching.

```APIDOC
CheckItem Extension Placeholders:

%checkitem_<modifier1>,<modifier2>,<...>% 
  - Returns true if the user has the item matching the conditions.

%checkitem_amount_<modifier1>,<modifier2>,<...>% 
  - Returns the amount of items the user has matching the conditions.

%checkitem_remove_<modifier1>,<modifier2>,<...>% 
  - Removes items from the player's inventory. Can be used with amount (e.g., %checkitem_amount_remove_<...>%).
  - WARNING: This action permanently removes items.
  - Note: Disabled by default; enable in PlaceholderAPI config.yml.

%checkitem_give_<modifier1>,<modifier2>,<...>% 
  - Gives items to the player. Returns true if successful, or the number of items NOT given if unsuccessful.
  - Note: Disabled by default; enable in PlaceholderAPI config.yml.

%checkitem_getinfo:<slot>_<modifier1>,<modifier2>,<...>% 
  - Returns information about an item in a specific slot. Information is returned in the order listed on the wiki, separated by " &r".
  - Supported slots: `mainhand`, `offhand`, or numerical slot index.


Modifiers (can be combined):

namecontains:<text>
  - Checks if the item's display name contains <text> (case-sensitive).

namestartswith:<text>
  - Checks if the item's display name starts with <text> (case-sensitive).

nameequals:<text>
  - Checks if the item's display name equals <text> (case-sensitive).

mat:<material>
  - Checks if the item is of a specific <material> (e.g., STONE).

amt:<number>
  - Checks if the player has <number> of items.

data:<number>
  - Checks if the item has data <number> (e.g., WOOL:14 for Red wool). (1.12 and older only)

custommodeldata:<number>
  - Checks if the item has CustomModelData <number>. (1.14 and newer only)

lorecontains:<text>
  - Checks if the item's lore contains <text> (case-sensitive).
  - With `getinfo`, <text> can be a number to specify a lore line.

loreequals:<text>
  - Checks if the item's lore equals <text>. Lines are separated by `|`.
  - With `getinfo`, <text> can be a number to specify a lore line.

matcontains:<text>
  - Checks if the item's material contains <text> (case-sensitive).

enchantments:<enchantment=lvl>;<enchantment>
  - Checks if the item's enchantments contain <enchantment> with an optional level. Uses vanilla Minecraft enchantment names.

enchanted
  - Checks if the item is enchanted (with anything).

potiontype:<potiontype>
  - Checks if the item has the specified <potiontype>.

potionextended:<boolean>
  - Checks if a potion is extended.

potionupgraded:<boolean>
  - Checks if a potion is upgraded.

strict
  - Requires `mat:` modifier. Strictly checks the name and lore for an item (e.g., `%checkitem_mat:stone,strict%` will return false for a Stone named 'Test').

inhand
  - Checks if the item is in the player's hand (both hands). Can specify `:main` or `:off` (e.g., `inhand:main`).

inslot:<number>
  - Checks if the item is in a specific slot.

nbtstrings:<key>=<value>;<key>=<value>
  - Checks if the item's nbtStrings contains <key> with the value of <value> (case-sensitive).
  - Supports compounds using `..` (e.g., `nbtstrings:PublicBukkitValues..executableitems:ei-id=Free_Money`).

nbtints:<key>=<value>;<key>=<value>
  - Checks if the item's nbtInts contains <key> with the value of <value> (case-sensitive).
  - Supports compounds using `..`.


General Notes:
- To use commas in strings, escape them using `\` (e.g., `loreequals:Milk\, Eggs\, Bread`).
- Placeholders are supported within modifier values but must be wrapped in curly brackets (e.g., `{player_name}`).
```

--------------------------------

### Enchantment PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Documentation for the Enchantment expansion, providing placeholders to retrieve information about enchantments on a player's held item, including lists and individual enchantment levels.

```APIDOC
%enchantment_list%
  - Returns a comma-separated list of enchantments on the item a player is holding.
%enchantment_listLF%
  - Returns a newline-separated list of enchantments on the item a player is holding.
%enchantment_roman_list%
  - Returns a comma-separated list of enchantments on the item a player is holding, with levels in Roman numerals.
%enchantment_roman_listLF%
  - Returns a newline-separated list of enchantments on the item a player is holding, with levels in Roman numerals.
%enchantment_<enchantment_name>_level%
  - Returns the level of the specified enchantment on the item a player is holding.
    - <enchantment_name>: The name of the enchantment (e.g., 'sharpness', 'protection').
```

### Formatter PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

This expansion offers placeholders for comprehensive number and text formatting. It supports number formatting with locales and patterns, time unit conversions, rounding with precision and modes, and shortening numbers. For text, it provides functions for length, lowercase, uppercase, substring extraction, and string replacement.

```PlaceholderAPI
%formatter_number_format_<number>%
%formatter_number_format_[locale]:[pattern]_<number>%

%formatter_number_from:<timeunit>_to:<timeunit>_<number>% # Converts <number> from one time unit to another

%formatter_number_round_<number>%
%formatter_number_[precision]:[roundingmode]_<number>%

%formatter_number_shorten_<number>%

%formatter_number_time_<number>%
%formatter_number_time_<timeunit>_<number>% # Handles number as <timeunit>

%formatter_text_length_<text>%

%formatter_text_lowercase_<text>%

%formatter_text_replace_[target]_[replacement]_<text>%

%formatter_text_substring_[start]:[end]_<text>%

%formatter_text_uppercase_<text>%
```

### LocalTime PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Documentation for the LocalTime expansion, enabling display of current local time with customizable formats and support for specific time zones using SimpleDateFormat patterns and TimeZone IDs.

```APIDOC
%localtime_time%
  - Displays the current local time in a default format.
%localtime_time_<SimpleDateFormat>%
  - Displays the current local time formatted according to the SimpleDateFormat pattern.
    - <SimpleDateFormat>: A valid Java SimpleDateFormat pattern (e.g., 'HH:mm:ss', 'yyyy-MM-dd').
%localtime_timezone_<TimeZoneID>%
  - Displays the current time for a specific time zone.
    - <TimeZoneID>: A valid Java TimeZone ID (e.g., 'America/New_York', 'Europe/London').
%localtime_timezone_<TimeZoneID>,<SimpleDateFormat>%
  - Displays the current time for a specific time zone, formatted according to the SimpleDateFormat pattern.
    - <TimeZoneID>: A valid Java TimeZone ID.
    - <SimpleDateFormat>: A valid Java SimpleDateFormat pattern.
```

### PlaceholderAPI Shortcut Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Enables parsing of large amounts of text, including other PlaceholderAPI placeholders, by referencing predefined shortcuts from a specified filename. Supports optional replacements within the shortcut.

```PlaceholderAPI
%shortcut_<filename>% 
%shortcut_<filename>:<replacement1>:replacement2>:...%
```

--------------------------------

### AngelChest Plugin Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Placeholders for the AngelChest plugin, providing information about active AngelChests, associated costs for actions like spawning, teleporting, fetching, and opening, and status checks for specific chests by ID.

```APIDOC
%angelchest_activechests% - Number of active chests a player has
%angelchest_price% - Amount of money needed to spawn the AngelChest
%angelchest_price_teleport% - Amount of money needed to teleport to the AngelChest
%angelchest_price_fetch% - Amount of money needed to teleport the AngelChest to yourself
%angelchest_price_open% - Amount of money needed to open the AngelChest
%angelchest_enabled% - returns "true" or "false" whether this player has AngelChest enabled
%angelchest_isactive_<ID>% - Returns "true" when the player has an AngelChest with this ID, otherwise "false"
%angelchest_time_<ID>% - Remaining time for this AngelChest. Returns an empty String if no AngelChest with this ID exists.
```

### Javascript PlaceholderAPI Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/Placeholders

Documentation for the Javascript expansion, providing a way to use Javascript code within placeholders to generate dynamic and conditional output.

```APIDOC
%javascript_<your placeholder identifier>%
  - Executes the Javascript code associated with the identifier, allowing for conditional output.
```

--------------------------------

### GraalJS PlaceholderAPI Integration

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

This expansion allows integration with GraalJS, enabling dynamic placeholder output based on JavaScript logic. It supports passing arguments to the underlying JavaScript code, allowing for flexible and conditional data generation. The <your_placeholder_identifier> refers to a defined script.

```PlaceholderAPI
%graaljs_<your_placeholder_identifier>%
%graaljs_<your_placeholder_identifier>_arg1%
%graaljs_<your_placeholder_identifier>_arg1,arg2,arg3%
```

### Register PlaceholderAPI Expansion in Bukkit Plugin

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/PlaceholderExpansion

This Java code snippet illustrates how to register a custom PlaceholderAPI expansion within a Bukkit `JavaPlugin`. The `onEnable()` method checks for the presence of PlaceholderAPI and, if found, instantiates and registers the custom expansion, making its placeholders available for use.

```Java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example

import org.bukkit.Bukkit;
import org.bukkit.plugin.java.JavaPlugin;

public class SomePlugin extends JavaPlugin {
    
    @Override
    public void onEnable() {
        // Small check to make sure that PlaceholderAPI is installed
        if(Bukkit.getPluginManager().getPlugin("PlaceholderAPI") != null) {
              new SomeExpansion(this).register();
        }
    }
}
```

### Registering a Custom PlaceholderAPI Expansion in Bukkit (Java)

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/PlaceholderExpansion

This snippet shows how to register a custom PlaceholderAPI expansion within a Bukkit plugin. It demonstrates checking for PlaceholderAPI's presence and then instantiating and registering the SomeExpansion class in the plugin's onEnable method. This ensures the expansion is active when the plugin loads.

```java
package at.helpch.placeholderapi.example;

import org.bukkit.Bukkit;
import org.bukkit.plugin.java.JavaPlugin;

public class SomePlugin extends JavaPlugin {
    
    @Override
    public void onEnable() {
        // Small check to make sure that PlaceholderAPI is installed
        if(Bukkit.getPluginManager().getPlugin("PlaceholderAPI") != null) {
              new SomeExpansion(this).register();
        }
    }
}
```

### Javascript PlaceholderAPI Integration

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

This expansion provides a mechanism to use JavaScript for generating dynamic placeholder output. It allows for conditional results based on custom JavaScript logic, offering flexibility beyond standard placeholders. The <your placeholder identifier> links to a specific JavaScript implementation.

```PlaceholderAPI
%javascript_<your placeholder identifier>%
```

### Sack Plugin Placeholders

Source: https://github.com/placeholderapi/placeholderapi/wiki/temp_wiki_06579f44c797ae903e1c8d72358bb1c9bdeca778/Placeholders

Placeholders for the Sack plugin, providing dynamic details about a player's held sacks, including name, slot information, and item counts. To use these, download the plugin via `/papi ecloud download Sack`.

```APIDOC
%sack_name% : the name of the sack you're holding.
%sack_slots% : the number of slots available for the sack you're holding.
%sack_num_of_sacks% : the number of sacks the palyer currently has in its inventory.
%sack_empty_slots% : the number of empty slots in the sack you're holding.
%sack_filled_slots% : the number of slots, which have some items.
%sack_total_slots% : the total number of slots for all sacks in your inventory.
%sack_total_item_count% : the total number of items held in all sacks in your inventory.
```
